Appendix B
Preliminary and background

We provide an overview of the preliminary results used in this work. For self-contained completeness, we include results on asymptotic deterministic equivalence in
Section

B.1
, results on ridge regression in
Section

B.2
, and results on random feature ridge regression in
Section

B.3
. Additionally,
Section

B.4
presents results on non-asymptotic deterministic equivalence, along with definitions of quantities required for these results. Finally,
Section

B.5
introduces key results for deriving the scaling law.

B.1
Asymptotic deterministic equivalence

For the ease of description, we include preliminary results on asymptotic deterministic equivalence here. In fact, these assumptions and results can be recovered from non-asymptotic results, e.g.,
[
37
]
.

For linear regression, the asymptotic deterministic equivalence aim to find
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\sim{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
,
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\sim{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
, where
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
and
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
are some deterministic quantities.
For asymptotic results, a series of assumptions in high-dimensional statistics via random matrix theory are required, on well-behaved data, spectral properties of
\bm{\Sigma}
under nonlinear transformation in high-dimensional regime.
We put the assumption from
[
1
]
here that are also widely used in previous literature
[
15
,
51
]
.

Assumption 3
.

[
1
, Well-behaved data]

We assume that:

(A1)

The sample size
n
and dimension
d
grow to infinity with
\frac{d}{n}\to\gamma>0
.

(A2)

{\bm{X}}={\bm{T}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}
, where
{\bm{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
has i.i.d. sub-Gaussian entries with zero mean and unit variance.

(A3)

{\bm{\Sigma}}
is invertible with
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\text{op}}<\infty
and its spectral measure
\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\delta_{\sigma_{i}}
converges to a compactly supported probability distribution
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

(A4)

\|{\bm{\beta}}_{\ast}\|_{2}<\infty
and the measure
\sum_{i=1}^{d}({\bm{v}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{\ast})^{2}\delta_{\sigma_{i}}
converges to a measure
\nu
with bounded mass, where
{\bm{v}}_{i}
is the unit-norm eigenvector of
{\bm{\Sigma}}
related to its respective eigenvalue
\sigma_{i}
.

Definition B.1

(Effective regularization)
.

For
n
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}
, and
\lambda\geq 0
, we define the
effective regularization

\lambda_{*}
to be the unique non-negative solution to the self-consistent equation

n-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{*}}\sim{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}).

(7)

Definition B.2

(Degrees of freedom)
.

{\rm df}_{1}(\lambda_{*}):={\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}),\quad{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*}):={\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2}).

Proposition B.3
.

[
1
, Restatement of Proposition 1]

Assume
(A1)
,
(A2)
,
(A3)
, we consider
{\bm{A}}
and
{\bm{B}}
with bounded operator norm, admitting the convergence of the empirical measures, i.e.,
\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{v}}_{i}\cdot\delta_{\sigma_{i}}\rightarrow\nu_{A}

and
\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{B}}{\bm{v}}_{i}\cdot\delta_{\sigma_{i}}\rightarrow\nu_{B}
with bounded total variation, respectively. Then, for
\lambda\geq 0
, with
\lambda_{*}
satisfying Eq. (
7
),
we have the following
asymptotic deterministic equivalence

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1})\sim

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})\,,

(8)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{B}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1})\sim

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}{\bm{B}}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})

\displaystyle+\lambda_{*}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2}{\bm{\Sigma}})

\displaystyle\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{B}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2}{\bm{\Sigma}})\cdot\frac{1}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*})}\,,

(9)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1})\sim

\displaystyle~\frac{\lambda_{*}}{\lambda}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})\,,

(10)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{B}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1})\sim

\displaystyle~\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}{\bm{B}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2}{\bm{\Sigma}})

\displaystyle\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{B}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2}{\bm{\Sigma}})\cdot\frac{1}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*})}\,.

(11)

Proposition B.4
.

[
1
, Restatement of Proposition 2]

Assume
(A1)
,
(A2)
,
(A3)
, we consider
{\bm{A}}
and
{\bm{B}}
with bounded operator norm, admitting the convergence of the empirical measures, i.e.,
\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{v}}_{i}\cdot\delta_{\sigma_{i}}\rightarrow\nu_{A}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{B}}{\bm{v}}_{i}\cdot\delta_{\sigma_{i}}\rightarrow\nu_{B}
with bounded total variation, respectively. Then, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}{\bm{a}}ckslash\mathbb{R}_{+}
, with
\lambda_{*}
satisfying Eq. (
7
), we have the following
asymptotic deterministic equivalence

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}({\bm{T}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{T}})\sim

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}),

(12)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}({\bm{T}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{T}}{\bm{B}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}({\bm{T}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{T}})\sim

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}{\bm{B}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})

\displaystyle+\lambda_{*}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2})

\displaystyle~\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{B}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*})}\,.

(13)

Note that the results in
Proposition

B.3
,
B.4
still hold even for the random features model.
We will explain this in details in
Appendix

E
.

B.2
Deterministic equivalence for ridge regression

We consider
n
samples
\{{\bm{x}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
sampled i.i.d. from a distribution
\mu_{\bm{x}}
over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}:=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{x}}{\bm{x}}^{\!\top}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
.
The label
y_{i}
is generated by a linear target function parameterized by
\bm{\beta}_{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e.,
y_{i}={\bm{x}}_{i}^{\!\top}\bm{\beta}_{\ast}+\varepsilon_{i}
, where
\varepsilon_{i}
is additive noise independent of
{\bm{x}}_{i}
satisfying
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}]=0
and
\text{var}(\varepsilon_{i})=\sigma^{2}
.
We can write the model in a compact form as
{\bm{y}}={\bm{X}}\bm{\beta}_{\ast}+\bm{\varepsilon}
, where the data matrix as
{\bm{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
, the label vector
{\bm{y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and the noise vector as
\bm{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
The estimator of ridge regression is given by
\hat{\bm{\beta}}=\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{y}}
.
We also consider min-
\ell_{2}
-norm solution in the over-parameterized regime, i.e.,
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{\min}=\argmin_{\bm{\beta}}\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2},\text{s.t. }{\bm{X}}\bm{\beta}={\bm{y}}
.
The excess risk of
\hat{\bm{\beta}}
admits a bias-variance decomposition

\mathcal{R}^{\tt LS}:=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\|\bm{\beta}_{*}-\hat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}\!=\!{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\|\bm{\beta}_{*}-\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[\hat{\bm{\beta}}]\|_{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\mathrm{Cov}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}))}\,,

where the first RHS term is the
bias
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}
, and the second term is the
variance
, denoted by
\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}
. Accordingly, the bias-variance decomposition is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}^{\tt LS}:=

\displaystyle~\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}]\|_{{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{2}=\lambda^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle\,,

(14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}^{\tt LS}:=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}\mathrm{Cov}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}})\right)=\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})\,.

(15)

Under proper assumptions (to be detailed later), we have the following deterministic equivalents, asymptotically
[
1
]
and non-asymptotically
[
10
]

\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}\!\sim\!\mathsf{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathsf{R},\lambda}\!:=\!\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2})}\,,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}\!\sim\!\mathsf{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathsf{R},\lambda}\!:=\!\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-2})}\,,

(16)

where
\lambda_{*}
is the non-negative solution to the self-consistent equation
n-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{*}}={\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\mathbf{I}})^{-1})
.

Accordingly, the risk admits the following deterministic equivalents via bias-variance decomposition.

Proposition B.5
.

[
1
, Restatement of Proposition 3]

Given the bias variance decomposition in
Eq.

14
and
Eq.

15
,
{\bm{X}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}
satisfy
Assumption

3
, we have the following asymptotic deterministic equivalents
\mathcal{R}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}\sim{\mathsf{R}}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}:={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
such that
\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}\sim{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
,
\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}\sim{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
, where
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
and
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
are defined by
Eq.

16
.

Proposition B.6
.

[
1
, Restatement of results in Sec 5]

Under the same assumption as
Proposition

B.5
, for the minimum
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}
, we have for the under-parameterized regime (
d<n
):

\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{R},0}^{\tt LS}=0,\quad\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{R},0}^{\tt LS}\sim\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{n-d}\,.

In the over-parameterized regime (
d>n
), we have

\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{R},0}^{\tt LS}\sim\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}\,,\qquad\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{R},0}^{\tt LS}\sim\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}\,,

where
\lambda_{n}
defined by
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim n
.

B.3
Deterministic equivalence for random feature ridge regression

Recall
Eq.

1
, the parameter
\bm{a}
can be learned by the following empirical risk minimization with an
\ell_{2}
regularization

\hat{{\bm{a}}}:=\argmin_{{\bm{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\bm{a}_{j}\varphi(\bm{x},\bm{w}_{j})\right)^{2}+\lambda\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{y}}\,.

Assuming that the target function
f_{*}\in L^{2}(\mu_{\bm{x}})
admits
f_{*}({\bm{x}})=\sum_{k\geq 1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*,k}\psi_{k}({\bm{x}})
, the excess risk
\mathcal{R}^{\tt RFM}:={\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{*}-\frac{{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}\hat{{\bm{a}}}}{\sqrt{p}}\right\|_{2}^{2}
admits the following bias-variance decomposition

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}:=

\displaystyle~\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{*}-\frac{{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[\hat{{\bm{a}}}]}{\sqrt{p}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\left\|{\bm{\theta}}_{*}-p^{-1/2}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}\bm{\theta}_{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\,,

(17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}:=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}\mathrm{Cov}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{{\bm{a}}})\right)=\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}(\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})\,.

(18)

Accordingly, the risk admits the following deterministic equivalents via bias-variance decomposition.

Proposition B.7
.

[
14
, Asymptotic version of Theorem 3.3]

Given the bias variance decomposition in
Eq.

17
and
Eq.

18
,
under
Assumption

1
, we have the following asymptotic deterministic equivalents
\mathcal{R}_{\lambda}^{\tt RFM}\sim{\mathsf{R}}_{\lambda}^{\tt RFM}:={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}
such that
\mathcal{B}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}\sim{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}
,
\mathcal{V}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},\lambda}\sim{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}
, where
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}
and
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}
are defined by
Eq.

2
.

Note that the above results are delivered in a non-asymptotic way
[
14
]
, but more notations and technical assumptions are required. We give an overview of non-asymptotic deterministic equivalence as below.

B.4
Non-asymptotic deterministic equivalence

Regarding non-asymptotic results, we require a series of notations and assumptions. We give a brief introduction here for self-completeness. More details can be found in
[
10
,
37
,
14
]
.

Given
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the associated covariance matrix is given by
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{x}}{\bm{x}}^{\!\top}]
. We denote the eigenvalue of
{\bm{\Sigma}}
in non-increasing order as
\sigma_{1}\geq\sigma_{2}\geq\sigma_{3}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{d}
.

We introduce the non-asymptotic version of
Eq.

7
as below.

Definition B.8

(Effective regularization)
.

Given
n
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}
, and
\lambda\geq 0
, the
effective regularization

\lambda_{*}
is defined as the unique non-negative solution of the following self-consistent equation

n-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{*}}={\rm Tr}\big({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}\big).

Remark:

Existence and uniqueness of
\lambda_{*}
are guaranteed since the left-hand side of the equation is monotonically increasing in
\lambda_{*}
, while the right-hand side is monotonically decreasing.

In the next, we introduce the following definitions on “effective dimension”, a metric to describe the model capacity, widely used in statistical learning theory.

Define
r_{{\bm{\Sigma}}}(k):=\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{\geq k})}{\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\geq k}\|_{\rm op}}=\frac{\sum_{j=k}^{d}\sigma_{j}}{\sigma_{k}}
as the intrinsic dimension, we require the following definition

\rho_{\lambda}(n):=1+\frac{n\sigma_{\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot n\rfloor}}{\lambda}\left\{1+\frac{r_{{\bm{\Sigma}}}(\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot n\rfloor)\vee n}{n}\log\big(r_{{\bm{\Sigma}}}(\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot n\rfloor)\vee n\big)\right\},

(19)

where
\eta_{*}\in(0,1/2)
is a constant that will only depend on
C_{*}
defined in
Assumption

4
. And we used the convention that
\sigma_{\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot n\rfloor}=0
if
\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot n\rfloor>d
.

In this section we consider functionals that depend on
{\bm{X}}
and deterministic matrices. For a general PSD matrix
{\bm{A}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
, define the functionals

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda):=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\right),

(20)

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda):=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}\right),

(21)

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda):=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\right),

(22)

\displaystyle\Phi_{4}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda):=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}\frac{{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}}{n}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\right).

(23)

These functionals can be approximated through quantities that scale proportionally to

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}}):=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-1}\right),

(24)

\displaystyle\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}}):=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}{n-{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}.

(25)

The following theorem gathers the approximation guarantees for the different functionals stated above, and is obtained by modifying
[
14
, Theorem A.2]
.
We generalize
Eq.

28
for any PSD matrix
\bm{A}
, which will be required for our results on the deterministic equivalence of
\ell_{2}
norm. The proof can be found in
Appendix

C
.

Theorem B.9

(Dimension-free deterministic equivalents, Theorem A.2 of
[
14
]
)
.

Assume the features
\{{\bm{x}}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
satisfy
Assumption

4
with a constant
C_{*}>0
. Then for any
D,K>0
, there exist constants
\eta_{*}\in(0,1/2)
,
C_{D,K}>0
and
C_{*,D,K}>0
ensuring the following property holds. For any
n\geq C_{D,K}
and
\lambda>0
, if the following condition is satisfied:

\lambda\cdot\rho_{\lambda}(n)\geq\|\bm{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot n^{-K},\quad\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}\log^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(n)\leq K\sqrt{n},

(26)

then for any PSD matrix
{\bm{A}}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
, we have that

\displaystyle|\Phi_{1}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda)-\frac{\lambda_{*}}{\lambda}\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}\log^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{*}}{\lambda}\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}}),

(27)

\displaystyle|\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{I}},\lambda)-\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{I}})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{4}\log^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{I}}),

(28)

\displaystyle|\Phi_{3}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda)-\left(\frac{n\lambda_{*}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\left(\frac{n\lambda_{*}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}}),

(29)

\displaystyle|\Phi_{4}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda)-\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}}).

(30)

Next, we present some of the concepts to be used in deriving random feature ridge regression. Similar to how ridge regression depends on
\lambda_{*}
, as defined in
Definition

B.8
, the deterministic equivalence of random feature ridge regression relies on
\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}
, which are the solutions to the coupled equations

n-\frac{\lambda}{\nu_{1}}={\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2})^{-1}\right)\,,\quad p-\frac{p\nu_{1}}{\nu_{2}}={\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2})^{-1}\right).

(31)

Writing
\nu_{1}
as a function of
\nu_{2}
produces the equations as below

1+\frac{n}{p}-\sqrt{\left(1-\frac{n}{p}\right)^{2}\!+\!4\frac{\lambda}{p\nu_{2}}}=\frac{2}{p}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2})^{-1}\right),\nu_{1}\!:=\!\frac{\nu_{2}}{2}\left[1-\frac{n}{p}+\sqrt{\left(1-\frac{n}{p}\right)^{2}+4\frac{\lambda}{p\nu_{2}}}\right].

(32)

For random features, our results also depend on the capacity of
{\bm{\Lambda}}
. Recall the definition of
r_{\bm{\Lambda}}(k):=\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}{\geq k})}{\|{\bm{\Lambda}}{\geq k}\|_{\rm op}}
as the intrinsic dimension of
{\bm{\Lambda}}
at level
k
, we sequentially define the following quantities that can be found in
[
37
,
14
]
.

\displaystyle M_{\bm{\Lambda}}(k)=

\displaystyle~1+\frac{r_{{\bm{\Lambda}}}(\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot k\rfloor)\vee k}{k}\log\left(r_{{\bm{\Lambda}}}(\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot k\rfloor)\vee k\right)\,,

(33)

\displaystyle\rho_{\kappa}(p)=

\displaystyle~1+\frac{p\cdot\xi^{2}_{\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot p\rfloor}}{\kappa}M_{\bm{\Lambda}}(p)\,,

(34)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}_{\kappa}(n,p)=

\displaystyle~1+\mathbbm{1}\{n\leq p/\eta_{*}\}\cdot\left\{\frac{n\xi_{\lfloor\eta_{*}\cdot n\rfloor}^{2}}{\kappa}+\frac{n}{p}\cdot\rho_{\kappa}(p)\right\}M_{\bm{\Lambda}}(n)\,,

(35)

where the constant
\eta_{*}\in(0,1/2)
only depends on
C_{*}
introduced in Assumption
1
.

For an integer
{\mathsf{m}}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we split the covariance matrix
{\bm{\Lambda}}
into low degree part and high degree part as

{\bm{\Lambda}}_{0}:={\rm diag}(\xi_{1}^{2},\xi_{2}^{2},\ldots,\xi_{{\mathsf{m}}}^{2})\,,\quad{\bm{\Lambda}}_{+}:={\rm diag}(\xi_{{\mathsf{m}}+1}^{2},\xi_{{\mathsf{m}}+2}^{2},\ldots)\,.

After we define the high degree feature covariance
{\bm{\Lambda}}_{+}
, we can define the function
\gamma(\kappa):=\kappa+{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}_{+})
. To simplify the statement, we assume that we can choose
{\mathsf{m}}
such that
p^{2}\xi_{{\mathsf{m}}+1}^{2}\leq\gamma(p\lambda/n)
, which is always satisfied under
Assumption

1
. For convenience, we will further denote

\gamma_{+}:=\gamma(p\nu_{1}),\quad\quad\gamma_{\lambda}:=\gamma(p\lambda/n).

(36)

For random feature ridge regression, we will first demonstrate that the
\ell_{2}
norm concentrates around a quantity that depends only on
\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}
. To this end, we define the following functionals with respect to
{\bm{Z}}
.

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}({\bm{Z}};{\bm{A}},\kappa)

\displaystyle:={\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}^{1/2}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\kappa)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\kappa)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}^{1/2}\right),

(37)

\displaystyle\Phi_{4}({\bm{Z}};{\bm{A}},\kappa)

\displaystyle:={\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}^{1/2}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\kappa)^{-1}\frac{{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}}{n}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\kappa)^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}^{1/2}\right).

Given that
{\bm{Z}}
consists of i.i.d. rows with covariance
\widehat{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\bm{F}}={\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}/p
, we will demonstrate that the aforementioned functionals can be approximated by those of
{\bm{F}}
, which, in turn, can be represented using the following functionals:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{5}({\bm{F}};{\bm{A}},\kappa)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\Phi}_{6}({\bm{F}};{\bm{A}},\kappa)}{n-\widetilde{\Phi}_{6}({\bm{F}};\bm{I},\kappa)},

(38)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{6}({\bm{F}};{\bm{A}},\kappa)

\displaystyle:={\rm Tr}\left({\bm{A}}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{2}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+\kappa)^{-2}\right).

Proposition B.10

(Deterministic equivalents for
\Phi({\bm{Z}})
conditional on
{\bm{F}}
, Proposition B.6 of
[
14
]
)
.

Assume
\{{\bm{z}}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
and
\{{\bm{f}}\}_{i\in[p]}
satisfy
Assumption

1
with a constant
C_{*}>0
, and
{\bm{F}}\in\mathcal{A}_{{\bm{F}}}
defined in
[
14
, Eq. (79)]
. Then for any
D,K>0
, there exist constants
\eta_{*}\in(0,1/2)
,
C_{D,K}>0
and
C_{*,D,K}>0
ensuring the following property holds. Let
\rho_{\kappa}(p)
and
\tilde{\rho}_{\kappa}(n,p)
be defined as per
Eq.

34
and
Eq.

35
,
\gamma_{+}
be defined as
Eq.

36
. For any
n\geq C_{D,K}
and
\lambda>0
, if the following
condition is satisfied:

\displaystyle\lambda\geq n^{-K}\,,\quad\quad\widetilde{\rho}_{\lambda}(n,p)^{5/2}\log^{3/2}(n)\leq K\sqrt{n}\,,\quad\quad\widetilde{\rho}_{\lambda}(n,p)^{2}\cdot\rho_{\gamma_{+}}(p)^{5/2}\log^{3}(p)\leq K\sqrt{p}\,,

then for any PSD matrix
{\bm{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
(independent of
{\bm{Z}}
conditional on
{\bm{F}}
), we have with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
that

\displaystyle\left|\Phi_{3}({\bm{Z}};{\bm{A}},\lambda)-\left(\frac{n\nu_{1}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{5}({\bm{F}};{\bm{A}},p\nu_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{1}(n,p)\cdot\left(\frac{n\nu_{1}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{5}({\bm{F}};{\bm{A}},p\nu_{1}),

(39)

\displaystyle\left|\Phi_{4}({\bm{Z}};{\bm{A}},\lambda)-\widetilde{\Phi}_{5}({\bm{F}};{\bm{A}},p\nu_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{1}(n,p)\cdot\widetilde{\Phi}_{5}({\bm{F}};{\bm{A}},p\nu_{1}),

(40)

where the rate
\mathcal{E}_{1}(n,p)
is given by
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(n,p):=\frac{\widetilde{\rho}_{\lambda}(n,p)^{6}\log^{5/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\widetilde{\rho}_{\lambda}(n,p)^{2}\cdot\rho_{\gamma_{+}}(p)^{5/2}\log^{3}(p)}{\sqrt{p}}
.

B.5
Scaling law

For the derivation of the scaling law, we use the results in
[
14
, Appendix D]
. We define
T^{s}_{\delta,\gamma}(\nu)
as

T^{s}_{\delta,\gamma}(\nu):=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k^{-s-\delta\alpha}}{(k^{-\alpha}+\nu)^{\gamma}}\,,\quad s\in{0,1},\;0\leq\delta\leq\gamma.

Under
Assumption

2
, according to
[
14
, Appendix D]
, we have the following results

T_{\delta\gamma}^{s}(\nu)=O\left(\nu^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{\alpha}}\left[s-1+\alpha(\delta-\gamma)\right]\wedge 0}\right).

(41)

Next, we present some rates of the quantities used in the deterministic equivalence of random feature ridge regression. The rate of
\nu_{2}
is given by

\nu_{2}\approx O\left(n^{-\alpha\left(1\wedge q\wedge\nicefrac{{\ell}}{{\alpha}}\right)}\right),

(42)

and in particular, for
\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
and
\chi(\nu_{2})
, we have

1-\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})=O(1)\,,

(43)

\chi(\nu_{2})=n^{-q}O\left(\nu_{2}^{-1-\nicefrac{{1}}{{\alpha}}}\right)\,.

(44)

Appendix C
Proofs on additional non-asymptotic deterministic equivalents

In this section, we aim to generalize
Eq.

28
for any PSD matrix
\bm{A}
, i.e.

\big|\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})-\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\big|\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})\cdot\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\,,

that is required to derive our non-asymptotic deterministic equivalence for the bias term of the
\ell_{2}
norm.

By introducing a change of variable
\mu_{*}:=\mu_{*}(\lambda)=\lambda/\lambda_{*}
, we find that
\mu_{*}
satisfies the following fixed-point equation:

\displaystyle\mu_{*}=\frac{n}{1+{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}(\mu_{*}{\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda)^{-1})}.

(45)

We define
{\bm{t}}
and
{\bm{T}}
as follows

{\bm{t}}={\bm{\Sigma}}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\bm{x}}\,,\quad{\bm{T}}={\bm{X}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,.

And the following resolvents are also defined

{\bm{R}}:=({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}\,,\quad\overline{{\bm{R}}}:=(\mu_{*}{\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda)^{-1}\,,\quad{\bm{M}}:={\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\bm{R}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,,\quad\overline{{\bm{M}}}:={\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\overline{{\bm{R}}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

Since the proof relies on a leave-one-out argument, we define
{\bm{X}}_{-}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-1)\times d}
as the data matrix obtained by removing one data. We also introduce the associated resolvent and rescaled resolvent:

{\bm{R}}_{-}:=({\bm{X}}_{-}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}_{-}+\lambda)^{-1},\overline{{\bm{R}}}_{-}:=\left(\frac{n}{1+\kappa}{\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda\right)^{-1},{\bm{M}}_{-}:={\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\bm{R}}_{-}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}},\overline{{\bm{M}}}_{-}:={\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\overline{{\bm{R}}}_{-}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}},

where
\kappa={\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{M}}_{-})]
.

For the sake of narrative convenience, we introduce a functional used in
[
37
]

\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}}):={\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}(\mu_{*}{\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda)^{-1})\,.

Next, we give the proof of
Eq.

28
. We consider the functional

\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})={\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})={\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}}).

Remark:
Note that, to align more closely with the proof in
[
37
]
, the
\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})
defined here differs slightly from the
\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda)
in
Eq.

28
. However, the two definitions are equivalent if we take
{\bm{A}}
here as
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\Sigma}}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\bm{B}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
, which recovers the formulation in
Eq.

28
.

We show that
\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})
is well approximated by the following deterministic equivalent:

\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})={\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\mu_{*}{\bm{\Sigma}}(\mu_{*}{\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda)^{-1})={\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}).

Theorem C.1

(Deterministic equivalent for
{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})
)
.

Assume the features
\{{\bm{x}}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
satisfy
Assumption

4
with a constant
C_{*}>0
. Then for any
D,K>0
, there exist constants
\eta\in(0,1/2)
,
C_{D,K}>0
, and
C_{*,D,K}>0
ensuring the following property holds. For any
n\geq C_{D,K}
and
\lambda>0
, if the following condition is satisfied:

\lambda\cdot\rho_{\lambda}(n)\geq n^{-K},\qquad\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{2}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}(n)\leq K\sqrt{n},

(46)

then for any PSD matrix
{\bm{A}}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
, we have that

\big|\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})-\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\big|\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{4}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}}).

(47)

Remark:

Theorem

C.1
generalizes
Eq.

28
. Note that there are some differences between
\rho_{\lambda}
as defined in
Eq.

19
and
\nu_{\lambda}
as defined in
[
37
]
. However, based on the discussion in
[
14
, Appendix A]
,
\nu_{\lambda}
can be easily adjusted to match
\rho_{\lambda}
. Therefore, while we follow the argument in
[
37
]
, we use
\rho_{\lambda}
directly in this work to minimize additional notation.

Following the approach outlined in
[
37
]
, our proof involves separately bounding the deterministic and martingale components. This is accomplished in the following two propositions.

Proposition C.2

(Deterministic part of
{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})
)
.

Under the same assumption as
Theorem

C.1
, there exist constants
C_{K}
and
C_{*,K}
, such that for all
n\geq C_{K}
and
\lambda>0
satisfying
Eq.

46
, and for any PSD matrix
{\bm{A}}
, we have

\big|{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})]-\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\big|\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{4}}{\sqrt{n}}\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}}).

(48)

Proposition C.3

(Martingale part of
{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})
)
.

Under the same assumption as
Theorem

C.1
, there exist constants
C_{K,D}
and
C_{*,D,K}
, such that for all
n\geq C_{K,D}
and
\lambda>0
satisfying
Eq.

46
, and for any PSD matrix
{\bm{A}}
, we have with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
that

\big|\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})-{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})]\big|\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{3}\log^{\frac{3}{2}}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}}).

(49)

Theorem
C.1
is obtained by combining the bounds (
48
) and (
49
). Next, we prove the two propositions above separately.

Proof of Proposition
C.2
.

First, by Sherman-Morrison identity

{\bm{M}}={\bm{M}}_{-}-\frac{{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{t}}{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}}{1+{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{t}}}\,,\quad\text{and}\quad{\bm{M}}{\bm{t}}=\frac{{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{t}}}{1+{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{t}}}\,,

we decompose
{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})]
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})\right]=

\displaystyle n{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}}}{1+S}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\frac{{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-})]}{1+\kappa}+n{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\kappa-S}{(1+\kappa)(1+S)}{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}}\right],

where we denoted
S={\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{t}}
. Therefore, bounding the following two terms is sufficient

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}})]-\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\right|

(50)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-})]}{1+\kappa}-\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\right|+\left|n{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\kappa-S}{(1+\kappa)(1+S)}{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}}\right]\right|.

For the first term, recall that
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
is the solution of the equation (
45
) where we replaced
n
by
n-1
, and
\tilde{\mu}_{-}:=n/(1+\kappa)
. By
[
37
, Proposition 2]
, we have

\left|{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-})]-\Psi_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{*};{\bm{A}})\right|\leq{\mathcal{E}}^{({\sf D})}_{1,n-1}\cdot\Psi_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{*};{\bm{A}})\,,

where
{\mathcal{E}}^{({\sf D})}_{1,n-1}=C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}}{\sqrt{n-1}}
. For
n\geq C
, we have
{\mathcal{E}}_{1,n-1}^{({\sf D})}\leq C{\mathcal{E}}_{1,n}^{({\sf D})}
and by
[
37
, Lemma 3]
, we have

|\Psi_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{*};{\bm{A}})-\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})|\leq C\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)}{n}\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\,.

Combining the above bounds, we obtain

\left|{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-})]-\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\right|\leq{\mathcal{E}}^{({\sf D})}_{1,n}\cdot\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\,.

Furthermore, from the proof of
[
37
, Proposition 4, Claim 3]
, we have

\frac{|\mu_{*}-\tilde{\mu}_{-}|}{\tilde{\mu}_{-}}\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Then we conclude that

\displaystyle|\mu_{*}-\tilde{\mu}_{-}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\tilde{\mu}_{-}

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\left(1+C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\mu_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\mu_{*},

where we use condition (
46
) in the last inequality.

Combining this inequality with the previous bounds, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{n{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-})]}{1+\kappa}-\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\mu}_{-}{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-})]-\mu_{*}\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{-}\left|{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-})]-\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\right|+\frac{|\tilde{\mu}_{-}-\mu_{*}|}{\mu_{*}}\cdot\mu_{*}\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C{\mathcal{E}}_{1,n}^{({\sf D})}\cdot\mu_{*}\Psi_{1}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C{\mathcal{E}}_{1,n}^{({\sf D})}\cdot\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\,.

In the next, we aim to estimate the second term in Eq. (
50
). Here we can reduce
{\bm{A}}
to be a rank-one matrix
{\bm{A}}:=\bm{v}\bm{v}^{{\!\top}}
following
[
37
, Eq. (77)]
. We simply apply Hölder’s inequality and obtain

\displaystyle~n\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\kappa-S}{(1+\kappa)(1+S)}{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|\frac{\kappa-S}{(1+\kappa)(1+S)}{\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{v}}{\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{t}}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~n\mathbb{E}_{\bm{M}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[(\kappa-S)^{2}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{v}}{\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{t}})^{2}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~n\mathbb{E}_{\bm{M}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[(\kappa-S)^{2}\right]\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{v}}{\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{t}})^{2}\right]\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~n\mathbb{E}_{\bm{M}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[(\kappa-S)^{2}\right]\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{v}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\bm{t}}\left[({\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{t}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

Each of these terms can be bounded, according to the proof of
[
37
, Proposition 2]
, for the first term, we get

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{t}}-\kappa)^{2}\right]\right]^{1/2}\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}.

For the second term, first according to
[
37
, Lemma 2]
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}_{\bm{t}}\left[({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{v}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq C_{*,K}{\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}^{2}{\bm{v}}\,,

{\mathbb{E}}_{\bm{t}}\left[({\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{t}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq C_{*,K}{\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{v}}\,.

Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{v}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\bm{t}}\left[({\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{t}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[C_{*,K}{\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}^{2}{\bm{v}}{\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{v}}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle=C_{*,K}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-}^{2}{\bm{A}})\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

Then according to
[
37
, Lemma 4.(b)]
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{-}^{2}{\bm{A}})\right]\leq C_{*,K}\rho^{2}_{\lambda}(n){\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}}_{-}^{2}{\bm{A}})=C_{*,K}\rho^{2}_{\lambda}(n){\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}}_{-})^{2},

where the last inequality holds due to
{\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}}_{-}
being a rank-1 matrix. Combining the bounds for the second term, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{M}}_{-}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{t}}}\left[({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{v}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\bm{t}}\left[({\bm{v}}^{\!\top}{\bm{t}})^{4}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq C_{*,K}\rho_{\lambda}(n){\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}}_{-})\leq C_{*,K}\rho_{\lambda}^{2}(n){\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}}).

By combining the above bounds for the first and second term, we have

\displaystyle n\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\kappa-S}{(1+\kappa)(1+S)}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}^{3}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}n{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}^{4}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\mu_{*}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}}),

where we use
\mu_{*}=\frac{n}{1+{\rm Tr}(\overline{{\bm{M}}})}\geq\frac{n}{2\rho_{\lambda}(n)}
according to
[
37
, Lemma 3]
in the last inequality.

Combining the above bounds concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
Proposition

C.3
.

The martingale argument follows a similar approach to the proofs of
[
37
, Propositions 3 and 5]
. The key remaining steps are to adjust Step 2 in
[
37
, Proposition 3]
and establish high-probability bounds for each term in the martingale difference sequence.

We rewrite this term as a martingale difference sequence

\displaystyle S_{n}:={\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})-{\mathbb{E}}[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}_{i-1}\right){\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})=:\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\,,

where
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
is denoted as the expectation over
\{{\bm{x}}_{i+1},\cdots,{\bm{x}}_{n}\}
.

We show below that
|\Delta_{i}|\leq R
with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
with

R=C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{2}\log(n)}{n}\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}}).

(51)

For Step 3 and bounding
{\mathbb{E}}_{i-1}[\Delta_{i}\mathbbm{1}_{\Delta_{i}\not\in[-R,R]}]
, observe that with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
, by
[
37
, Lemma 4.(b)]

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{i-1}[\Delta_{i}^{2}]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{i-1}\left[\frac{({\bm{t}}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{-}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}})^{2}}{(1+S)^{2}}\right]^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{3}\log^{1/2}(n)}{n}\mu_{*}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{3}\log^{1/2}(n)}{n}\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}}).

We establish a high-probability bound for
\Delta_{i}
by first decomposing it and strategically adding and subtracting carefully chosen terms. Observing that

\Delta_{i}=\left({\mathbb{E}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}_{i-1}\right){\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})=\left({\mathbb{E}}_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}_{i-1}\right)\left({\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})-{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i})\right),

where
{\bm{M}}_{i}
is the rescaled resolvent removes
{\bm{x}}_{i}
, and we used that
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\left[{\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i}\right]={\mathbb{E}}_{i-1}\left[{\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i}\right]
, and we’ll write (recall that
S_{i}={\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{t}}_{i}
)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}{\bm{M}})-{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i})=

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{t}}_{i}{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}+{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}){\bm{M}})-{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}}_{i}+{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}})-{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{(1+S_{i})}\left\{{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}}_{i}-{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{t}}_{i}{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{(1+S_{i})}{\rm Tr}({\bm{t}}_{i}{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{A}}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i})).

Observing that

{\bm{I}}-{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i}=\lambda{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}{\bm{M}}_{i},

we can write for
j\in\{i-1,i\}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
,

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\left[\frac{1}{(1+S_{i})}{\rm Tr}({\bm{t}}_{i}{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{A}}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{T}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{T}}_{i}{\bm{M}}_{i}))\right]\right|\leq

\displaystyle\lambda{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\left[|{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{t}}_{i}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\mathbb{E}}_{j}\left[|{\bm{t}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{M}}_{i}{\bm{A}}{\bm{t}}_{i}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C_{*,D}\log(n){\mathbb{E}}_{j}\left[{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{M}}_{i})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C_{*,D}\rho_{\lambda}(n)\log(n){\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C_{*,D}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{2}\log(n)}{n}\mu_{*}{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}\overline{{\bm{M}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~C_{*,D}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{2}\log(n)}{n}\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}}),

where we used that
{\bm{M}}_{i}\preceq{\bm{\Sigma}}/\lambda
by definition in the second inequality,
[
37
, Lemma 4.(b)]
in the fourth inequality, and
\mu_{*}=\frac{n}{1+{\rm Tr}(\overline{{\bm{M}}})}\geq\frac{n}{2\rho_{\lambda}(n)}
in the last inequality.

Applying a union bound and adjusting the choice of
D
, we conclude that with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
, the following holds for all
i\in[n]
:

|\Delta_{i}|\leq C_{*,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{2}\log(n)}{n}\Psi_{2}(\mu_{*};{\bm{A}})\,.

∎

Appendix D
Main results and proofs for linear regression

In this section, we study the asymptotic and non-asymptotic deterministic equivalent of the (ridge/ridgeless) estimator norm for linear regression.
Based on these results, we are able to mathematically characterize the test risk under norm-based capacity.

Table

3
presents our main results for linear regression.

:

(power-law)

To deliver our results, we need the following lemma for the bias-variance decomposition of the estimator’s norm.

Lemma D.1

(Bias-variance decomposition of
\mathcal{N}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}
)
.

We have the bias-variance decomposition
{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}^{2}=:\mathcal{N}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}=\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}+\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}
, where
\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}:=\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}})^{2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle\,,\quad\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}:=\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})\,.

And we present the proof of
Lemma

D.1
as below.

Proof of
Lemma

D.1
.

Here we give the bias-variance decomposition of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}^{2}
. The formulation of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}\,,

which can be decomposed as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}({\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\bm{\varepsilon})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}})^{2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle+\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}+\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\,.

Accordingly, we can see that it shares the similar spirit with the bias-variance decomposition.
∎

Our first goal is to relate
\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}
to their respective deterministic equivalents. And next, we will present the results for both asymptotic and non-asymptotic regime separately.

D.1
Asymptotic deterministic equivalence for ridge regression

In this section, we establish the asymptotic approximation guarantees for linear regression, focusing on the relationships between the
\ell_{2}
norm of the estimator and its deterministic equivalent.
These results can be recovered by our non-asymptotic results, but we put them here just for completeness.

Before presenting the results on deterministic equivalence for ridge regression and their proofs, we begin by introducing a couple of useful corollaries from
Propositions

B.3
and
B.4
.

Corollary D.2
.

Under the same condition of
Proposition

B.3
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-2})\sim

\displaystyle~\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*})}\,.

(52)

Specifically, if
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\Sigma}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-2})\sim

\displaystyle~\frac{{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*})}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*})}\,.

(53)

Corollary D.3
.

Under the same condition of
Proposition

B.4
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}({\bm{T}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{T}}^{\!\top}+\lambda)^{-2}{\bm{T}})\sim

\displaystyle~\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2})}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{*})}\,.

(54)

Using the equation

{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-2})=\frac{1}{\lambda}\left({\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1})-{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}})^{2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-2})\right)\,,

we can directly obtain
Corollaries

D.2
and
D.3
from
Propositions

B.3
and
B.4
.

Now we are ready to derive the deterministic equivalence, i.e.,
{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}^{2}
, under the bias-variance decomposition. Our results can handle ridge estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}
in
Proposition

D.4
and interpolator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}
in
Corollary

D.5
, respectively.

Proposition D.4

(Asymptotic deterministic equivalence of
\mathcal{N}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
.)
.

Given the bias variance decomposition of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}^{2}
in
Lemma

D.1
,
under
Assumption

3
, we have the following asymptotic deterministic equivalents
\mathcal{N}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\sim{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}:={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
such that
\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}\sim{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
,
\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}\sim{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
, where these quantities are from
Lemma

D.1
and
Eq.

55
.

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}:=

\displaystyle~\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle+{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\underbrace{\hbox to209pt{\vbox to13.84pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#B30000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#B30000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}:=

\displaystyle~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}\underbrace{\hbox to185.88pt{\vbox to13.84pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#0000B3} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#0000B3} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{}}{}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{92.93736pt}{6.92001pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 128.6 9.58)} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{}}{}
