\lxSVG@closescope {}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#0000B3} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{1.0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity=1.0} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#E6E6FF} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{1.0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity=1.0} \lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.83 0.83 L 0.83 18.32 L 256.36 18.32 L 256.36 0.83 Z}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {
{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 18.6 L 257.19 18.6}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{}}{}{
{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.55 L 257.19 0.55}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{1.0}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity=1.0} {{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.6pt}{4.1pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 2.21 5.67)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}_{{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}}}\,.

(55)

We remark that, by checking
Eq.

16
and
Eq.

55
,
norm-based capacity suffices to characterize generalization while effective dimension can not
, where effective dimension is defined as
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-1})

[
63
]
or similar formulation, e.g.,
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}^{2}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})
.

•

Bias: for the bias term, we find that the second term of
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
rescales
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
in
Eq.

16
by a factor
{\color[rgb]{0.7,0,0}\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n}}
.

•

Variance: we find that the variance term of the norm
{\mathsf{V}}^{\tt LS}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}
equals the variance term of the test risk
{\mathsf{V}}^{\tt LS}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}
in
Eq.

16
multiplied by a factor
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.7}\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}}
. That means, under isotropic features
\bm{\Sigma}={\bm{I}}_{d}
, they are the same.

Accordingly, the norm-based capacity is able to characterize the bias and variance of the excess risk.
We provide a quantitative analysis of this relationship in
Section

D.3.2
.
Below, we present the proof of
Proposition

D.4
.

Proof of
Proposition

D.4
.

We give the asymptotic deterministic equivalents for
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
, respectively. For the bias term
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
, we use
9
by taking
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}
and
{\bm{B}}={\bm{I}}
and thus obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}})^{2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}})^{2}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle+\lambda_{*}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle+\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\,.

For the variance term
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N}}^{\tt LS}
, we use
Eq.

52
by taking
{\bm{A}}={\bm{I}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N}}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})\sim\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}=:{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\,.

∎

The deterministic equivalent of the norm for the min-
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator.

We have the following results on the characterization of the deterministic equivalence of
\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}\|_{2}
.

Corollary D.5

(Asymptotic deterministic equivalence of the norm of interpolator)
.

Under
Assumption

3
, for the minimum
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}
, we have the following deterministic equivalence: for the under-parameterized regime (
d<n
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},0}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,,\quad\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},0}\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n-d}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})\,.

In the over-parameterized regime (
d>n
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},0}\sim

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\tt LS}_{\mathcal{N},0}\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{n}}\,,

where
\lambda_{n}
is defined by
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim n
.

Remark:
The asymptotic behavior of
\lambda_{*}
differs between the under-parameterized and over-parameterized regimes as
\lambda\to 0
, though the ridge regression estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}
converges to the min-
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}
.
To be specific, in the under-parameterized regime,
\lambda_{*}
converges to
0
as
\lambda\to 0
; while in the over-parameterized regime,
\lambda_{*}
converges to a constant that admits
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim n
when
\lambda\to 0
.
Accordingly, for the minimum
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator, it is necessary to analyze the two regimes separately. And we show that the solution
\lambda_{n}
to the self-consistent equation
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim n
can be obtained from the variance
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\sigma^{2}/\lambda_{n}
.

Proof of
Corollary

D.5
.

We separate the results in the under-parameterized and over-parameterized regimes.

In the under-parameterized regime (
d<n
), for minimum norm estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}
, we have (for
{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}
is invertible)

\displaystyle\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}=\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{y}}=\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}({\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\bm{\varepsilon})={\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\left({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}\right)^{-1}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}\bm{\varepsilon}\,.

Accordingly, we can directly obtain the bias-variance decomposition as well as their deterministic equivalents

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt LS}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,,\quad\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt LS}=\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}})^{-2})\sim\sigma^{2}\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})}{n-d}\,,

where we use
Eq.

52
and take
\lambda\to 0
for the variance term.

In the over-parameterized regime (
d>n
), we take the limit
\lambda\to 0
within ridge regression and use
Proposition

D.4
.
Define
\lambda_{n}
as
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim n
, we have for the bias term

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt LS}\sim

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle+\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle-\lambda_{n}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle\,.

For the variance term, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt LS}\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}\,.

Finally we conclude the proof.
∎

D.2
Non-asymptotic analysis on the deterministic equivalents of estimator’s norm

To derive the non-asymptotic results, we make the following assumption on well-behaved data.

Assumption 4

(Data concentration
[
37
]
)
.

There exist
C_{*}>0
such that for any PSD matrix
{\bm{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with
{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma A})<\infty
and
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{X}}-{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma A})\right|\geq t\|{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)\leq C_{*}e^{-\frac{t}{C_{*}}}\,.

Assumption 5

(
[
14
]
)
.

There exists
C>1

\frac{\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}\leq C\,.

Remark:

This assumption holds in many settings of interest, such as power law assumptions like those in
Assumption

6
, since under this assumption the numerator and denominator are bounded sums of finite terms. It is a technical assumption used to address the difference between two deterministic equivalents that are needed in our work for norm-based capacity.
In fact, this assumption is used for RFMs in
[
14
]
as the authors also face with the issue on the difference between two deterministic equivalents.

Based on the above two assumptions, we are ready to deliver the following result, our results can also numerically validated by
Fig.

11
in
Section

H.2
.

Theorem D.6

(Deterministic equivalents of the
\ell_{2}
-norm of the estimator.)
.

Assume well-behaved data
\{\bm{x}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
satisfy
Assumption

4
and
Assumption

5
. Then for any
D,K>0
, there exist constants
\eta_{*}\in(0,1/2)
and
C_{*,D,K}>0
ensuring the following property holds. For any
n\geq C_{*,D,K}
,
\lambda>0
, if the following condition is satisfied:

\lambda\geq n^{-K}\,,\quad\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}\log^{3/2}(n)\leq K\sqrt{n}\,,

then with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}-{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\right|\leq

\displaystyle C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\,,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}-{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\right|\leq

\displaystyle C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\,.

Next, we give the proof of
Proposition

D.4
below.

Proof of
Theorem

D.6
.

Part 1: Deterministic equivalents for the bias term.

Here we prove the deterministic equivalents of
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
. First, we decompose
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
into

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}

\displaystyle={\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-1}\right)-\lambda{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}({\bm{X}}^{\!\top}{\bm{X}}+\lambda)^{-2}\right),

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1},\lambda)-n\lambda\Phi_{4}({\bm{X}};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2},\lambda)\,,

where
\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}
,
\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2}:={\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1/2}
.
Therefore, using
Theorem

B.9
, with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\Phi_{2}({\bm{X}};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1},\lambda)-\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{5/2}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\left|n\lambda\Phi_{4}({\bm{X}};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2},\lambda)-n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})\,.

Combining the above bounds, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}-\left(\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1})-n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1})+n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})\right).

Note that

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1})-n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\,.

For
n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})
, recall that
\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}}):=\frac{1}{n}\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}
, and according to
Definition

B.8

and
Assumption

5
, we have

\displaystyle n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})=

\displaystyle\lambda\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{*}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-1})-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{C}\right){\rm Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})\,,

and therefore

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1})+n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})\leq

\displaystyle\left(2-\frac{1}{C}\right){\rm Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(2C-1\right)\frac{1}{C}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*})^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(2C-1\right)\left(\Psi_{1}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{1})-n\lambda\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};\tilde{{\bm{A}}}_{2})\right).

Then we conclude that

\left|\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}-{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\right|\leq C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS},

with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
.

Part 2: Deterministic equivalents for the variance term.
Next, we prove the deterministic equivalent of
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
. First, note that
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
can be written in terms of the functional
\Phi_{4}({\bm{X}};{\bm{A}},\lambda)
defined in
Eq.

23

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}=n\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Phi_{4}({\bm{X}};{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1},\lambda)\,.

Thus, under the assumptions, we can apply
Theorem

B.9
to obtain that with probability at least
1-n^{-D}

\left|n\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Phi_{4}({\bm{X}};{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1},\lambda)-n\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})\right|\leq C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}n\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})\,.

Recall that
\Psi_{2}(\lambda_{*};{\bm{A}}):=\frac{1}{n}\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{A}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{*}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}
, then we have

\left|\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt LS}-{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\right|\leq C_{x,D,K}\frac{\rho_{\lambda}(n)^{6}\log^{3/2}(n)}{\sqrt{n}}{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}\,,

with probability at least
1-n^{-D}
.
∎

D.3
Characterization of learning curves

By deriving deterministic equivalents for the norm in linear regression, we can now analyze learning curves through the lens of norm-based capacity. In certain cases, these learning curves can even be expressed in closed form.

In this section, we first examine the general characteristics of learning curves from a norm-based capacity perspective in
Section

D.3.1
. We then provide a precise characterization of these curves in
Section

D.3.2
.

D.3.1
The shape description of learning curves

We plot the bias and variance components of the test risk over
\gamma:=\frac{d}{n}
and norm, see
Fig.

4(a)
and
Fig.

4(b)
, respectively. Note that, our theory (shown in curve) can precisely predict experimental results (shown by points).

Fig.

4(a)
reveals a clear bias-variance tradeoff in the over-parameterized regime (where
\gamma>1
, as shown in the right portion of
Fig.

4(a)
). Specifically, we observe that:
i)
The bias exhibits a strictly increasing relationship with the parameter
\gamma
.
ii)
The variance demonstrates a corresponding strictly decreasing trend.

However, in the under-parameterized regime (
\gamma<1
), both bias and variance increase monotonically with
\gamma
, therefore the bias-variance tradeoff does not exist. In particular, for the min-
\ell_{2}
-norm interpolator, since the bias equals 0, the risk is entirely composed of variance.

Because the self-consistent equation differs between the under- and over-parameterized regimes, the learning curve plotted against the norm (see
Fig.

4(b)
) is not single-valued—this is due to the phase transition between regimes. Specifically, a single norm value can correspond to two distinct error levels, depending on whether the model is under- or over-parameterized. However, when examined separately, each regime displays a one-to-one relationship between test risk and norm.

(a)

Test risk vs.
\gamma

(\frac{d}{n})

(b)

Test risk vs. Norm

Figure 4
:
The relationship between the test risk
\mathsf{R}
, norm
\mathsf{N}
, their bias and variance (
\mathsf{B}_{\mathsf{R}}
,
\mathsf{V}_{\mathsf{R}}
,
\mathsf{B}_{\mathsf{N}}
,
\mathsf{V}_{\mathsf{N}}
), and the ratio
\gamma:=\frac{d}{n}
for linear regression model. Training data
\{({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
,
d=1000
, sampled from a linear model
y_{i}={\bm{x}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\varepsilon_{i}
,
\sigma^{2}=0.0004
,
{\bm{x}}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,{\bm{\Sigma}})
, with
\sigma_{k}({\bm{\Sigma}})=k^{-1}
,
{\bm{\beta}}_{*,k}=k^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}
. The ridge
\lambda=0.005
. Note that in the under-parameterized regime (
d<n
), the bias of the test risk is zero.

D.3.2
Mathematical formulation of learning curves

In this section, we give the mathematical formulation of learning curves in several settings of interest. First we give some concrete examples on the relationship between
{\mathsf{R}}
and
{\mathsf{N}}
in terms of isotropic features.

Proposition D.7

(Isotropic features for ridge regression, see
Fig.

5
)
.

Consider covariance matrix
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
, the deterministic equivalents
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\!-\!{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\!-\!{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\right)\left(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\!+\!{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\!-\!{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\right)^{2}d\!+\!2\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\left(\left(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\!+\!{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\!-\!{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\right)^{2}\!-\!4\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\right)\lambda\!=\!2\left(\left({\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\!-\!{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\right)^{2}\!-\!\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{4}\right)d\sigma^{2}\,.

Remark:

{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
formulates a third-order polynomial.
When
\lambda\to\infty
, it degenerates to
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}=(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}-\sqrt{{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}})^{2}
when
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}\leq\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Hence
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
is monotonically decreasing with respect to
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
, empirically verified by
Fig.

5
.
Besides, if we take
\lambda=\frac{d\sigma^{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}}
, which is the
optimal regularization parameter
discussed in
[
57
,
39
]
, the relationship in
Proposition

D.7
will become
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
, which corresponds to a straight line. This is empirically shown in
Fig.

5
with
\lambda=50
. In addition to isotropic features, we further examine the relationship under the power-law assumption for the data.

Apart from sufficiently large
\lambda
and optimal
\lambda
mentioned before, below we consider min-
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator.
Note that when
\lambda\to 0
, the ridge regression estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}
converges to the min-
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{\min}
.
However, the behavior of
\lambda_{*}
differs between the under-parameterized and over-parameterized regimes as
\lambda\to 0
. Thus, the min-
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator requires
separate analysis of the two regimes
.

Figure 5
:
Relationship between
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}
under the linear model
y_{i}={\bm{x}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\varepsilon_{i}
, with
d=500
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
,
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}=10
, and
\sigma^{2}=1
. The dashed line corresponds to the ridgeless regression curve.

Proof of
Proposition

D.7
.

According to the formulation of
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
and
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
in
Eq.

55
, for
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
, we have

{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}=\frac{1}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{d}{n(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\frac{1}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\frac{d}{n(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}\,,\quad{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}{n-\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}\,,

{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}=\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{d}{n(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\frac{d}{n(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}{n-\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}\,,

where
\lambda_{*}
admits a closed-form solution

\lambda_{*}=\frac{d+\lambda-n+\sqrt{4\lambda n+(n-d-\lambda)^{2}}}{2n}\,.

Recall the formulation
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
and
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}
(for test risk) in
Eq.

16
, for
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\frac{1}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\frac{d}{n(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}\,,\quad{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},\lambda}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}{n-\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}\,,\quad{\mathsf{R}}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\lambda_{*}^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\frac{d}{n(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}{n-\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{*})^{2}}}\,.

Accordingly, to establish the relationship between
{\mathsf{R}}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}
and
{\mathsf{N}}_{\lambda}^{\tt LS}
, we combine their formulation and eliminate
n
to obtain
5
5

5

Due to the complexity of the calculations, we use Mathematica Wolfram to eliminate
n
. The same approach is applied later whenever
n
or
p
elimination is required.

\displaystyle 2(({\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}-{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda})^{2}-\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{4})d\sigma^{2}=

\displaystyle(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}-{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda})(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}-{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda})^{2}d

\displaystyle+2\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}((\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda}-{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda})^{2}-4\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{\lambda})\lambda\,.

∎

Corollary D.8

(Isotropic features for min-
\ell_{2}
-norm interpolator, see
Fig.

5
)
.

Consider covariance matrix
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
, the relationship between
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
from under-parameterized to over-parameterized regimes admit

{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\begin{aligned} &{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}-\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,,~~\text{if}~~d<n~\mbox{(under-parameterized)};\\
&\sqrt{\left[{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}-(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2})\right]^{2}+4\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}-\sigma^{2}\,,\mbox{o/w}\,.\end{aligned}\end{array}\right.

For the variance part of
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
, we have
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}
; For the respective bias part, we have
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\|\bm{\beta}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}
.

Remark:

In the under-parameterized regime, the test error
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
is a linear function of the norm
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
.
In the over-parameterized regime,
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
formulates a rectangular hyperbola:
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
decreases with
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
if
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}<\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}
while
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
increases with
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
if
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}>\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}
.

Proof of
Corollary

D.8
.

According to
Proposition

B.6
and
Corollary

D.5
, for minimum
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator and
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
, for the under-parameterized regime (
d<n
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=0\,,\quad{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{n-d}\,;\quad\quad{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,,\quad{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{n-d}\,.

From these expressions, we can conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{n-d}\,;\quad\quad{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{n-d}\,.

Finally, in the under-parameterized regime, it follows that

{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}-\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

In the over-parameterized regime (
d>n
), the effective regularization
\lambda_{*}
will have an explicit formulation as
\lambda_{*}=\frac{d-n}{n}
, thus for the bias and variance of the test error, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-2})}=\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}\frac{1}{(1+\lambda_{n})^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}}{1-\frac{1}{n}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{n})^{2}}}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{d-n}{d}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}=\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{n})^{2}}}{n-\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{n})^{2}}}=\sigma^{2}\frac{n}{d-n}\,,

and combining the bias and variance, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{d-n}{d}+\sigma^{2}\frac{n}{d-n}\,.

(56)

For the bias and variance of the norm, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle=\frac{1}{1+\lambda_{n}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{n}{d}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}=\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{n})^{2}}}{n-\frac{d}{(1+\lambda_{n})^{2}}}=\sigma^{2}\frac{n}{d-n}\,,

and combining the bias and variance, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\frac{n}{d}+\sigma^{2}\frac{n}{d-n}\,.

(57)

Finally, combining
Eq.

56
and
Eq.

57
, we eliminate
n
and thus obtain

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}=\sqrt{({\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0})^{2}\!-\!2(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\!-\!\sigma^{2}){\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}+(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\!+\!\sigma^{2})^{2}}\!-\!\sigma^{2}\,.

By taking the derivative of
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}
with respect to
{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}
, we get

\frac{\partial{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}}{\partial{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}}=\frac{{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}-(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2})}{\sqrt{({\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS})^{2}-2(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}){\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}+(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}}\,.

From the derivative function, we observe that
{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}
decreases monotonically with
{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}
when
{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}<\|\bm{\beta}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}
, and increases monotonically with
{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}
when
{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}>\|\bm{\beta}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}
.
∎

Relationship for min-
\ell_{2}
-norm interpolator in the under-parameterized regime.

Next, we consider the min-norm estimator, and we find that for the min-norm estimator, in the under-parameterized regime, the relationship between risk and norm is linear, and this linearity is independent of the data distribution.

Proposition D.9

(Relationship for min-
\ell_{2}
-norm interpolator in the
under-parameterized
regime)
.

The deterministic equivalents
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
, in under-parameterized regimes (
d<n
) admit the linear relationship

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}={d}\left({\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}-\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\right)/{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})}\,.

Proof of
Proposition

D.9
.

According to
Proposition

B.6
and
Corollary

D.5
, for minimum
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator, in the under-parameterized regime (
d<n
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=0\,,\quad{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{n-d}\,;\quad\quad{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,,\quad{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})}{n-d}\,.

From these expressions, we can conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{n-d}\,;\quad\quad{\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}={\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})}{n-d}\,.

Finally, combing the above equation and eliminate
n
, in the under-parameterized regime, it follows that

{\mathsf{R}}_{0}^{\tt LS}=\frac{d}{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1})}\left({\mathsf{N}}_{0}^{\tt LS}-\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\,.

(58)

∎

The relationship in the over-parameterized regime is more complicated. We present it in the special case of isotropic features in
Corollary

D.8
of
Proposition

D.7
, and we also give an approximation in
Proposition

D.10
under the power-law assumption.

Assumption 6

(Power-law assumption)
.

For the covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
and the target function
\bm{\beta}_{*}
, we assume that
\sigma_{k}(\bm{\Sigma})=k^{-\alpha},\alpha>0
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{*,k}=k^{-\nicefrac{{\alpha\beta}}{{2}}},\beta\in\mathbb{R}
.

This assumption is close to classical source condition and capacity condition
[
9
]
and is similarly used in
[
45
, Assumption 1]
.

Relationship under power-law assumption.

Instead of assuming
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
, we next consider power-law features in
Assumption

6
and characterize the relationship.

Proposition D.10

(Power-law features for min-
\ell_{2}
norm estimator)
.

Under
Assumption

6
, in the over-parameterized regime (
d>n
), we consider some special cases for analytic formulation: if
\alpha=1
and
\beta=0
, when
n\to d
, we have
6
6

6

The symbol
\approx
here represents two types of approximations: i) approximation for self-consistent equations; ii) Taylor approximation of logarithmic function around zero (related to
n\to d
).

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}\approx\frac{2({\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})^{2}}{d{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}-d^{2}\sigma^{2}}\,,\quad{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}\!\approx\!\frac{2{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}(d-{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})}{d^{2}}\,.

Remark:

The relationship between
{\mathsf{R}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
and
{\mathsf{N}}^{\tt LS}_{0}
is still linear in the under-parameterized regime, but is quite complex in the over-parameterized regime.

Proof of
Proposition

D.10
.

In the over-parameterized regime (
d>n
), according to
Proposition

B.6
and
Corollary

D.5
, under
Assumption

6
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}=\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{1+\beta}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}{n-{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\rangle={\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{1+\beta}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}{n-{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}\,.

To compute these quantities, here we introduce the following continuum approximations to eigensums.

\int_{1}^{d+1}\frac{k^{-\alpha}}{k^{-\alpha}+\lambda_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}k\leq{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sigma_{i}+\lambda_{n}}\leq\int_{0}^{d}\frac{k^{-\alpha}}{k^{-\alpha}+\lambda_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}k\,,

(59)

due to the fact that the integrand is non-increasing function of
k
.
Similarly, we also have

\int_{1}^{d+1}\frac{k^{-2\alpha}}{(k^{-\alpha}+\lambda_{n})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}k\leq{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-2})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{(\sigma_{i}+\lambda_{n})^{2}}\leq\int_{0}^{d}\frac{k^{-2\alpha}}{(k^{-\alpha}+\lambda_{n})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}k\,.

(60)

We consider some special cases that are useful for discussion.
When
\alpha=1
, we have

\frac{\log(1+d\lambda_{n}+\lambda_{n})-\log(1+\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}}\leq{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1})\leq\frac{\log(1+d\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}}\,,

(61)

\frac{d+1}{\lambda_{n}d+\lambda_{n}+1}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}+1}\leq{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-2})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{(\sigma_{i}+\lambda_{n})^{2}}\leq\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}}\,.

(62)

Recall that
\lambda_{n}
is defined by
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})=n
. Using
Eq.

59
, we have

\frac{\log(1+d\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}}\approx n.

Observe that as
n\to d
,
\lambda_{n}\to 0
, allowing us to apply a Taylor expansion:

\frac{\log(1+d\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}}\approx\frac{d\lambda_{n}-\frac{1}{2}(d\lambda_{n})^{2}}{\lambda_{n}}=d-\frac{1}{2}d^{2}\lambda_{n}.

Based on this approximation,
\lambda_{n}
can be expressed as

\lambda_{n}\approx\frac{2(d-n)}{d^{2}}.

In the following discussion, we consider the case
n\to d
. Thus, we have the approximation

{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1})\approx n\,,\quad{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-2})\approx\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}}\,.

Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}{n-{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}\approx\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}}}{n-\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{n+d(n\lambda_{n}-1)}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}{n-{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}\approx\frac{\sigma^{2}\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}(d-\frac{1}{2}d^{2}\lambda_{n}-\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}})}{n-\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}d^{2}(d\lambda_{n}-1)}{2(n+d(n\lambda_{n}-1))}\,.

Use these two formulation to eliminate
n
, we obtain

{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}\approx\frac{2({\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})^{2}}{d{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}-d^{2}\sigma^{2}}\,.

Next we discuss the situation under different
\beta
.

For
\beta=0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}\approx\frac{\lambda_{n}(d-\frac{1}{2}d^{2}\lambda_{n}-\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}})}{1-\frac{d}{n(1+d\lambda_{n})}}=n\lambda_{n}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\approx d-\frac{1}{2}d^{2}\lambda_{n}\,,

Use these two formulation to eliminate
n
, we obtain

{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}\approx\frac{2{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}(d-{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})}{d^{2}}\,.

For
\beta=1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}\approx\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}\frac{d}{1+d\lambda_{n}}}{1-\frac{d}{n(1+d\lambda_{n})}}=\frac{nd\lambda_{n}^{2}}{n(1+d\lambda_{n})-d}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})={\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}})-\lambda_{n}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\approx{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}})-n\lambda_{n}\,.

Use these two formulation to eliminate
n
, we obtain

{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}\approx\frac{2\sqrt{({\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})^{2}-2{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}){\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}+{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}})^{2}}}{\sqrt{d^{2}+2d^{2}{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}-2d^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}})}}=\frac{2({\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}))}{d\sqrt{1+2{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}-2{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}})}}\,.

For
\beta=-1
, we need to use another two continuum approximations to eigensums

{\rm Tr}(({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}+\lambda_{n}}\approx\int_{0}^{d}\frac{1}{k^{-\alpha}+\lambda_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}k=\frac{d\lambda_{n}-\log(1+d\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}^{2}}\,,

{\rm Tr}(({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-2})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{(\sigma_{i}+\lambda_{n})^{2}}\approx\int_{0}^{d}\frac{1}{(k^{-\alpha}+\lambda_{n})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}k=\frac{\frac{d\lambda_{n}(2+d\lambda_{n})}{1+d\lambda_{n}}-2\log(1+d\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}^{3}}\,.

Once again, we apply the Taylor expansion, but this time expanding to the third order

\log(1+d\lambda_{n})\approx d\lambda_{n}-\frac{1}{2}(d\lambda_{n})^{2}+\frac{1}{3}(d\lambda_{n})^{3}\,.

Then we have

{\rm Tr}(({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1})\approx\frac{d\lambda_{n}-\log(1+d\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}^{2}}\approx\frac{1}{2}d^{2}-\frac{1}{3}d^{3}\lambda_{n}\,,

{\rm Tr}(({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n})^{-2})\approx\frac{\frac{d\lambda_{n}(2+d\lambda_{n})}{1+d\lambda_{n}}-2\log(1+d\lambda_{n})}{\lambda_{n}^{3}}=\frac{\frac{1}{3}d^{3}-\frac{2}{3}d^{4}\lambda_{n}}{1+d\lambda_{n}}\,.

Using the approximation sated above, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}{\rm Tr}(({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{1-n^{-1}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}\approx\frac{\lambda_{n}^{2}(\nicefrac{{(\frac{1}{3}d^{3}-\frac{2}{3}d^{4}\lambda_{n})}}{{(1+d\lambda_{n})}})}{1-\frac{d}{n(1+d\lambda_{n})}}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}(({\bm{\Sigma}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})=\frac{1}{2}d^{2}-\frac{1}{3}d^{3}\lambda_{n}\,.

Use these two formulation to eliminate
n
, we obtain

{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}\approx\frac{216({\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})^{4}\!-\!324d^{2}({\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})^{3}\!+\!126d^{4}({\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS})^{2}\!+\!d^{6}{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}\!-\!5d^{8}}{2d^{5}(6{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}-d^{2})}\,.

∎

Here we present some experimental results to check the relationship between
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}
and
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}
, as well as
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}
and
{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}
, see
Fig.

6
.
We can see that our approximate relationship on variance (see the
red
line in
Fig.

6(d)
) provides the precise estimation.
For the bias (see the left three figures of
Fig.

6
), our approximate relationship is accurate if
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{N}},0}^{\tt LS}
is large.

\beta=0

(b)

\beta=1

{\mathsf{V}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}
vs.

:
The left three figures (a) (b) (c) show the relationship between
{\mathsf{B}}_{{\mathsf{R}},0}^{\tt LS}
and

when
\alpha=1
and
\beta
takes on different values. The figure (d) shows the relationship between

when

. The

Appendix E
Proofs for random feature ridge regression

In this section, we provide the proof of deterministic equivalence for random feature ridge regression in both the asymptotic (
Section

E.1
) and non-asymptotic (
Section

E.2
) settings. Additionally, we provide the proof of the relationship between test risk and the
\ell_{2}
norm given in the main text, as detailed in
Section

E.3
.

Though the results
[
1
]
are for linear regression, we can still use the results for RFMs, which requires some knowledge from
Eqs.

28
and
27
.

We firstly confirm that
Assumption

3
in
Section

B.1
, used to derive all asymptotic results, can be replaced by the Hanson-Wright assumption employed in the non-asymptotic analysis.
It is evident that
Eqs.

8
and
10
are obtained directly by taking the limits of
Eqs.

28
and
27
as
n\to\infty
.

Additionally, a key step in the proof of
9
and
11
in
[
1
]
involves showing that
\Delta
is almost surely negligible, where
\Delta
is defined as

\Delta=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\bm{x}}_{i}{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\!\top}(\widehat{{\bm{\Sigma}}}_{-i}-z{\bm{I}})^{-1}-{\bm{\Sigma}}(\widehat{{\bm{\Sigma}}}-z{\bm{I}})^{-1}}{1+{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\!\top}(n\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{-i}-nz{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{x}}_{i}}\,,

with
\widehat{{\bm{\Sigma}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{x}}_{i}{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\!\top}
,
\widehat{{\bm{\Sigma}}}_{-i}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\neq i}{\bm{x}}_{j}{\bm{x}}_{j}^{\!\top}
, and
z\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

In the analysis of
[
1
]
, the negligibility of
\Delta
arises from the assumption that the components of
{\bm{x}}_{i}
follow a sub-Gaussian distribution, which leads to the Hanson-Wright inequality

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\!\top}{\bm{x}}_{i}-\mathrm{tr}({\bm{\Sigma}})\right|\leq c\left(t\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\sqrt{t}\|{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{F}\right)\right]\geq 1-2e^{-t}.

In this way,
Assumption

4
is also sufficient to establish the negligibility of
\Delta
.

After obtaining
Eqs.

8
and
10
and the negligibility of
\Delta
, we can follow the argument of
[
1
]
and derive the rest asymptotic deterministic equivalence.

Finally, with these observations, we can eliminate the reliance on
Assumption

3
and instead rely solely on
Assumption

4
to derive all the asymptotic results.

E.1
Asymptotic deterministic equivalence for random features ridge regression

In this section, we establish the asymptotic approximation guarantees for random feature regression in terms of its
\ell_{2}
-norm based capacity. Before presenting the proof of
Theorem

3.1
, we firstly give the proof of the bias-variance decomposition.

Proof.

Here we give the bias-variance decomposition of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{a}}}\|_{2}^{2}
. The formulation of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{a}}}\|_{2}^{2}
is given by

{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{a}}}\|_{2}^{2}={\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}\,,

which admits a similar bias-variance decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|\hat{{\bm{a}}}\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}+\bm{\varepsilon})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\varepsilon}\|({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle+\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}+\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}\,.

Accordingly, we conclude the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to present the proof of
Theorem

3.1
as below.

Proof of
Theorem

3.1
.

We give the asymptotic deterministic equivalents for the norm from the bias
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}
and variance
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}
, respectively. We provide asymptotic expansions in two steps, by first considering the deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{G}}
, and then over
{\bm{F}}
.

Under
Assumption

1
, we can apply
Propositions

B.3
,
D.2
,
B.4
and
D.3
directly in the proof below.

Deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{G}}
:

For the bias term, we use
13
in
Proposition

B.4
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{G}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}
and
{\bm{B}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
and obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}=&~\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle\\
=&~{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*})\\
=&~p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{G}})\\
\sim&~p\underbrace{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1})}_{\tt I_{1}}\\
&~+p\nu_{1}^{2}\underbrace{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}_{:=I_{2}}\cdot\underbrace{{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}_{:=I_{3}}\cdot\frac{1}{n-\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{1})}\,,\end{split}

(63)

where
\nu_{1}
defined by
\nu_{1}(1-\frac{1}{n}\widehat{\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{1}))\sim\frac{p\lambda}{n}
,
\widehat{\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{1})
and
\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{1})
are degrees of freedom associated to
{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
in
Definition

B.2
.

For the variance term, we use
Eq.

54
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{G}}
in
Proposition

B.4
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)=\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{Z}}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}({\bm{Z}}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}p{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}p\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{1})}\,.

Deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{F}}
:

In the next, we aim to eliminate the randomness over
{\bm{F}}
in
Eq.

63
from the bias part.
First our result depends on the asymptotic equivalents for
\widehat{\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{1})
and
\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{1})
. For
\widehat{\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{1})
, we use
Eq.

8
in
Proposition

B.3
with
{\bm{X}}={\bm{F}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{1})={\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-1})={\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{2})\,,

where
\nu_{2}
defined by
\nu_{2}(1-\frac{1}{p}{\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{2}))\sim\frac{\nu_{1}}{p}
. Hence
\nu_{1}
can be defined by
\nu_{1}(1-\frac{1}{n}{\rm df}_{1}(\nu_{2}))\sim\frac{p\lambda}{n}
from
Eq.

3
.

For
\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{1})
, we use
9
in
Proposition

B.3
with
{\bm{X}}={\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{B}}={\bm{I}}
and obtain

\begin{split}\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{1})&=~{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\\
&\sim~{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})+\nu_{2}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}\\
&=:~n\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})\,.\end{split}

(64)

For
I_{3}:={\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-2})
, we use
Eq.

52
with
{\bm{X}}={\bm{F}}
and obtain

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\sim

\displaystyle~{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}\,.

(65)

Then we use
Eq.

52
again with
{\bm{X}}={\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}
to obtain the deterministic equivalent of
I_{1}

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-1})=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}.

Further, for
I_{2}
, use
11
with
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}
and
{\bm{B}}={\bm{I}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\nu_{1}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\nu_{2}^{2}}{\nu_{1}^{2}}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{2}^{2}}{\nu_{1}^{2}}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\Lambda}})\cdot{\rm Tr}(({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\Lambda}})\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}.

Finally, combine the above equivalents, for the bias, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}\sim

\displaystyle p{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}

\displaystyle+p\nu_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{\nu_{2}^{2}}{\nu_{1}^{2}}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})+\frac{\nu_{2}^{2}}{\nu_{1}^{2}}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\Lambda}})\cdot\frac{{\rm Tr}(({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\Lambda}})}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}\cdot\frac{1}{n-n\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}

\displaystyle+\frac{p}{n}\left(\nu_{2}^{2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}+\nu_{2}^{2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{p-{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{p\chi(\nu_{2})}{n}\cdot\frac{\nu_{2}^{2}\left[\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle\!+\!\chi(\nu_{2})\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle\right]}{1-\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}\,.

Similarly, for the variance, using
Eq.

64
and
Eq.

65
for
I_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},\lambda}^{\tt RFM}\sim

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\nu_{2}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-{\rm df}_{2}(\nu_{2})}\cdot\frac{1}{n-n\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\frac{\frac{p}{n}\chi(\nu_{2})}{1-\Upsilon(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})}\,.

Accordingly, we finish the proof.
∎

In the next, we present the proof for min-
\ell_{2}
-norm interpolator under RFMs.

Proof of
Corollary

3.2
.

Similar to linear regression, we separate the two regimes
p<n
and
p>n
as well. For both of them, we provide asymptotic expansions in two steps, first with respect to
{\bm{G}}
and then
{\bm{F}}
in the under-parameterized regime and vice-versa for the over-parameterized regime.

Under-parameterized regime: Deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{G}}

For the variance term, we can use
Eq.

54
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{G}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot p{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot p{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{G}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot p{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{n-p}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot{\rm Tr}(({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1})\cdot\frac{p}{n-p}\,,

where
\tilde{\lambda}
is defined by

\tilde{\lambda}(1-\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\rm df}_{1}(\tilde{\lambda}))\sim\frac{p\lambda}{n}\,,

(66)

where
\widetilde{\rm df}_{1}(\tilde{\lambda})
and
\widetilde{\rm df}_{2}(\tilde{\lambda})
are degrees of freedom associated to
{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
. In the under-parameterized regime (
p<n
), when
\lambda
goes to zero, we have
\tilde{\lambda}\to 0
and
\widetilde{\rm df}_{2}(\tilde{\lambda})\to p

[
1
]
.

For the bias term, we use
13
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{G}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}
,
{\bm{B}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
and then obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{G}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-1})

\displaystyle+p\tilde{\lambda}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{n-p}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-2}{\bm{F}})+p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}}))\cdot{\rm Tr}(({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1})\cdot\frac{1}{n-p}\,.

In the next, we are ready to eliminate the randomness over
{\bm{F}}
.

Under-parameterized regime: deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{F}}

For the variance term, from
[
1
, Sec 3.2]
we know that
\nicefrac{{1}}{{\lambda_{p}}}
is almost surely the limit of
{\rm Tr}(({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1})
, thus we have

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}(({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1})\sim\frac{1}{\lambda_{p}}\,,

where
\lambda_{p}
defined by
{\rm df_{1}}(\lambda_{p})=p
, where
{\rm df_{1}}(\lambda_{p})
and
{\rm df_{2}}(\lambda_{p})
are degrees of freedom associated to
{\bm{\Lambda}}
. Hence we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}\sim\sigma^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda_{p}}\cdot\frac{p}{n-p}=\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{p}(n-p)}\,.

For the bias term, denote
{\bm{D}}:={\bm{F}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}
, we first use
Eq.

54
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{D}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{\Lambda}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
and obtain the deterministic equivalent of the first term in
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-2}{\bm{F}})

\displaystyle={\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top})^{-2}{\bm{D}})

\displaystyle\sim{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{p})}\,.

Then we use
13
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{D}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{\Lambda}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
and obtain

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}})={\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{D}})\sim{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1})\,,

Then the deterministic equivalent of the second term in
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}
is given by

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}}))\sim\lambda_{p}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}.

Finally, combine the above equivalents and we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}\sim

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{p}{n-{\rm df}_{2}(\lambda_{p})}+{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{p}{n-p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}+\frac{p\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{n-p}\,.

Over-parameterized regime: deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{F}}

Denote
{\bm{K}}:={\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
, for the variance term, we use
Eq.

54
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{D}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{A}}={\bm{K}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot p{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot p{\rm Tr}({\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{D}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot p{\rm Tr}({\bm{K}}({\bm{K}}+\hat{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-n}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot{\rm Tr}(({\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top})^{-1})\cdot\frac{p}{p-n}\,,

where
\hat{\lambda}
is defined by

\hat{\lambda}(1-\frac{1}{n}\widehat{\rm df}_{1}(\hat{\lambda}))\sim\frac{p\lambda}{n}\,,

(67)

where
\widehat{\rm df}_{1}(\hat{\lambda})
and
\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\hat{\lambda})
are degrees of freedom associated to
{\bm{K}}
. In the over-parameterized regime (
p>n
), when
\lambda
goes to zero, we have
\hat{\lambda}\to 0
and
\widehat{\rm df}_{2}(\hat{\lambda})\to n

[
1
]
.

For the bias term, we use
Eq.

54
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{D}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{K}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}=

\displaystyle p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{D}}({\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{D}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle p{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}({\bm{K}}+\hat{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-n}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{G}})\cdot\frac{p}{p-n}\,.

Over-parameterized regime: deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{G}}

For the variance term, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}\sim\sigma^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}\cdot\frac{p}{p-n}=\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{n}(p-n)}.

For the bias term, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{N},0}^{\tt RFM}\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1})\cdot\frac{p}{p-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{p}{p-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{p-n}\,.

Finally, we conclude the proof.
∎

To build the connection between the test risk and norm for the min-
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator for random features regression, we also need the deterministic equivalent of the test risk as below.

Proposition E.1

(Asymptotic deterministic equivalence of the test risk of the min-
\ell_{2}
-norm interpolator)
.

Under
Assumption

1
, for the minimum
\ell_{2}
-norm estimator
\hat{{\bm{a}}}_{\min}
, we have the following deterministic equivalence: for the under-parameterized regime (
p<n
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}\sim\frac{n\lambda_{p}\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{n-p}\,,\quad\mathcal{V}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{n-p}\,,

where
\lambda_{p}
is defined by
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim p
. In the over-parameterized regime (
p>n
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}\sim

\displaystyle\frac{n\lambda_{n}^{2}\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}+\frac{n\lambda_{n}\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{p-n}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}{n-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-2})}+\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{p-n}\,,

where
\lambda_{n}
is defined by
{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{n}{\bm{I}})^{-1})\sim n
.

Proof of
Proposition

E.1
.

For the proof, we separate the two regimes
p<n
and
p>n
. For both of them, we provide asymptotic expansions in two steps, first with respect to
{\bm{G}}
and then
{\bm{F}}
in the under-parameterized regime and vice-versa for the over-parameterized regime.

Under-parameterized regime: deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{G}}

For the variance term, in the under-parameterized regime, when
\lambda\to 0
, the variance term will become
\mathcal{V}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}=\sigma^{2}\cdot{\rm Tr}(\widehat{{\bm{\Lambda}}}_{{\bm{F}}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}})^{-1})
. Accordingly, using
[
1
, Eq. (12)]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot{\rm Tr}(\widehat{{\bm{\Lambda}}}_{{\bm{F}}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}})^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n-p}\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{n-p}\,.

For the bias term, it can be decomposed into

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}=

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\theta}}_{*}-p^{-1/2}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}-2p^{-1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}\widehat{{\bm{\Lambda}}}_{{\bm{F}}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}.

For the second term:
p^{-1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}
, we can use
Eq.

12
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{G}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
and obtain

\displaystyle p^{-1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{G}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-1})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}})\,,

where the implicit regularization parameter
\tilde{\lambda}
is defined by
Eq.

66
.

For the third term:
{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}\widehat{{\bm{\Lambda}}}_{{\bm{F}}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}
, we can use
13
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{G}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}
,
{\bm{B}}={\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}
and obtain

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}\widehat{{\bm{\Lambda}}}_{{\bm{F}}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{G}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-1})

\displaystyle+\tilde{\lambda}^{2}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot{\rm Tr}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}({\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}+\tilde{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{n-p}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}})+{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}}))\cdot\frac{p}{n-p}\,.

Combining the above equivalents, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}})+{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}}))\cdot\frac{p}{n-p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{n}{n-p}-{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}})\cdot\frac{n}{n-p}\,.

Under-parameterized regime: deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{F}}

For the bias term, we can use
Eq.

12
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{D}}:={\bm{F}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
and obtain

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{F}})=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{D}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\,.

Thus, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}\sim

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{n}{n-p}-{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{n}{n-p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{p}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\cdot\frac{n}{n-p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n\lambda_{p}\langle{\bm{\theta}}_{*},({\bm{\Lambda}}+\lambda_{p}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\rangle}{n-p}\,.

Over-parameterized regime: deterministic equivalent over
{\bm{F}}

For the variance term, with
{\bm{D}}:={\bm{F}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}
and
{\bm{K}}:={\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\tt RFM}_{\mathcal{R},0}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}(\widehat{{\bm{\Lambda}}}_{{\bm{F}}}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{F}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{D}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}{\bm{D}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-2})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{D}})\,,

then we directly use
13
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{D}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{K}}
,
{\bm{A}}={\bm{\Lambda}}
,
{\bm{B}}={\bm{K}}
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{D}})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{K}}+\hat{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{K}}({\bm{K}}+\hat{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-1})+\hat{\lambda}^{2}\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{K}}+\hat{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{K}}({\bm{K}}+\hat{\lambda}{\bm{I}})^{-2})\cdot\frac{1}{p-n}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top})^{-2}{\bm{G}})\!+\!\mathrm{Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}({\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top})^{-1}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}))\!\cdot\!\mathrm{Tr}(({\bm{G}}{\bm{\Lambda}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top})^{-1})\!\cdot\!\frac{1}{p-n}\,,

where the implicit regularization parameter
\hat{\lambda}
is defined by
Eq.

67
.

For the bias term, first we have

\displaystyle p^{-1/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}{\bm{Z}}+\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{Z}}^{\!\top}\bm{G}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}({\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{F}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{F}}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Tr}({\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{G}}^{\!\top}{\bm{G}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}({\bm{D}}{\bm{K}}{\bm{D}}^{\!\top}+p\lambda{\bm{I}})^{-1}{\bm{D}})\,,

then we use
Eq.

12
with
{\bm{T}}={\bm{D}}
,
{\bm{\Sigma}}={\bm{K}}
,
